# Solo-Owner Lane Claims

Some lanes must be driven **END-TO-END by ONE agent**. Splitting them across
agents causes exactly the failures this collaboration has hit repeatedly:
colliding edits, trampled landings, and *masked frontiers* (one agent's local
state hiding the real blocker from another). Those lanes are **SOLO lanes**.

Before working a SOLO lane you MUST claim it here. The claim is a git-atomic
lock: because claims are landed by fast-forward (`tools/ff_land.py`), exactly one
agent can win a claim race — the loser's push is refused and it backs off.

This registry is authoritative at `origin/main`. Read it there, not from a stale
checkout (`python tools/tree_drift_check.py --fetch` first).

## SOLO lanes (claim before starting)

- **`E1-WITNESS-TO-GREEN`** — the entire witness closure, owned end-to-end by one
  agent: seal-regen → WASM link → numpy static-lib link → execution →
  `candidate_outputs.npz` → `check_parity.py` PASS. Do NOT split this across
  agents; the seal artifacts, cpython-abi link, and acceptance lane are one
  coherent arc. (See the E1 order-of-operations in the PAUSE HANDOFF /
  `ORCHESTRATION.md`.)

- **`CODEX-B-CACHE-KEY`** — frontend lowering cache-KEY scoping (`cache_fingerprints.py`
  + `frontend_*` + aligned tests) so unrelated CLI/runtime/link edits don't cold-start
  the witness lowering cache. Coherent structural move; one owner. (Orchestrator-held;
  handoff from the prior in-progress agent per the reclaim directive in `ORCHESTRATION.md`.)

- **`REVIEW-5-SCCP-STR-UNICODE`** — adversarial-review finding #5 (P1
  CODEX-CORRECTNESS): SCCP constant-folding of str builtins/methods must use
  CPython code-point semantics, never Rust UTF-8 byte offsets. Single authority:
  `runtime/molt-passes/src/tir/passes/sccp/eval.rs`
  (`eval_concrete_builtin`/`eval_concrete_method`). One owner so the fix + teeth
  land as a coherent cut.

(Add new SOLO lanes here as the orchestrator or a claimant identifies them.)

## Protocol (binding)

**Helper (does steps 1-3 correctly for you):** `python tools/claim_lane.py <lane>
--check` (read-only; exit 0 = claimable, 1 = held → back off) and
`python tools/claim_lane.py <lane> --claim --agent <id> --note "..."` (pre-checks,
appends the row, `ff_land`s, and backs off on a lost race). Log progress/finish
with `--append PROGRESS|COMPLETE|RELEASED --agent <id> --note "..."`. The manual
steps below are the contract it implements.

1. **Drift-sweep first.** `git fetch origin`; read THIS file at current
   `origin/main`. `python tools/tree_drift_check.py --fetch` to confirm your tree
   isn't masking.
2. **Check the claim.** If the SOLO lane you want is `CLAIMED` and not STALE (see
   §6), **BACK OFF** — pick a different unclaimed SOLO lane, or a standing lane
   (Codex B/C/D). Never work a lane another agent holds.
3. **Claim it.** Append ONE row to the log (lane, your agent-id, UTC ISO
   timestamp, `CLAIMED`, first-step note) and land it with
   `python tools/ff_land.py`. Because landing is a fast-forward, exactly ONE
   claim wins a race. **If `ff_land` REFUSES (non-ff), someone moved first** —
   `git fetch`, re-read this file; if your lane is now claimed, discard your claim
   row and BACK OFF.
4. **Own it end-to-end.** While your claim is active, no other agent touches the
   lane's files, artifacts, or tests. Post append-only `PROGRESS` rows (with
   evidence: queue run ids, commits) at least every few hours so the claim is
   visibly alive.
5. **Final completion is NOT a green build.** Mark `COMPLETE` only after ALL of:
   - **(a) FINAL EXIT CRITERIA met, with evidence.** For `E1-WITNESS-TO-GREEN`:
     `candidate_outputs.npz` produced through Molt WASM AND `check_parity.py`
     PASS — cite the `pact-witness-acceptance` queue run id and paste the parity
     verdict. Zero fakes, no host-CPython/Pyodide fallback, POISON-clean.
   - **(b) RECURSIVE ADVERSARIAL REVIEW accomplished.** You (or a reviewer you
     spawn) actively tried to REFUTE the result from independent angles
     (re-run on a clean origin/main worktree; probe determinism/tolerances;
     confirm no masking, no fake symbol, no weakened assert) and it SURVIVED.
     Record what you tried and why it held.
   - **(c) SENIOR-ENGINEER REVIEW green.** Correctness incl. memory safety;
     performance (faster than CPython on the claimed target, honest numbers);
     CPython ≥3.12 compatibility in the verified subset; fidelity; no
     duplicate-authority / no partials. Record the sign-off.
   Only then append a `COMPLETE` row with the evidence links and `ff_land` it.
6. **Stale / release.** A claim with an explicit `RELEASED` row may be reclaimed.
   Otherwise a claim is STALE **only if BOTH** (a) no `PROGRESS` row for >4h AND
   (b) an OBJECTIVE-LIVENESS check shows no real activity. A missing PROGRESS row
   is NOT sufficient — an active agent often works silently in its worktree/queue
   without touching this log (this cost a wrong reclaim 2026-07-08). Before landing
   a `RECLAIM` row you MUST verify NO objective activity in the last ~2h:
   - the claimant's worktree commit log: `git -C <worktree> log --oneline --since="3 hours ago"`;
   - recent proof-queue rows for the lane: `tools/proof_queue.py status` (+ any
     worktree-local `logs/proof_queue`);
   - recent `origin/main` commits by that agent.
   If ANY show recent activity, the claim is ALIVE — do NOT reclaim (post a
   PROGRESS observation instead and ping the claimant to log its own progress).
   Cite the objective-liveness evidence (or its absence) in the `RECLAIM` row.
   Never silently take over a live claim; escalate to the orchestrator if contested.
   **Claimants:** post a `PROGRESS` row (or `python tools/claim_lane.py <lane>
   --append PROGRESS ...`) at least every few hours — silence forces others to
   guess your liveness from commits/queue, which is fragile.
7. **Self-retirement (terminal statuses).** A claim carries the seed of its OWN
   retirement (APPARATUS A11 / M11): besides the positive `COMPLETE` / `RELEASED`,
   a claimant may append an evidence-bearing **terminal** status that FREES the
   lane so stale custody cannot silently block a claimant. Recognized terminals:
   - `FALSIFIED` — the lane's PREMISE was disproven (cite what disproved it).
   - `MEASURED_IMPLEMENTATION_RETIRED` — built AND measured, then retired
     (superseded by a better result; cite the measurement).
   - `STALE_ASSUMED_DEAD` — no objective liveness after the CLAIMS.md §6 checks;
     retired with the liveness evidence (or its absence) cited.
   - `SUPERSEDED` — a different lane subsumed this one (cite the lane/commit).

   Append one via `python tools/claim_lane.py <lane> --append <STATUS> --agent
   <id> --note "<evidence>"`. `python tools/claims_status.py` reports the whole
   ledger's live-vs-retired counts and flags any stale `CLAIMED` lane (advisory,
   never an auto-reclaim); `python tools/check_sister_landed.py --files <targets>`
   is the premise-verification preflight a spawned agent runs BEFORE its first
   Write (exit 8 = STAND_DOWN_DUPLICATE, 9 = WAIT_AND_REASSESS) to avoid
   duplicating a landed lane or colliding with a mid-flight one (M22/M24).

| E1-WITNESS-TO-GREEN | claude/opus-e1-misaligned-ptr-20260710 | 2026-07-10T16:50:00Z | PROGRESS | MISALIGNED-POINTER CLASS FIX LANDED (a98ef2978e, ff on origin/main from fefa53c468; drift+fail_closed+table_drift gates PASS). This is the PRECISE root of row 194's "RuntimeError: unreachable" raw-wasm-trap frontier: iteration mode RUN 20260710T155049 (debug assertions ON) aborted at `runtime/molt-cpython-abi/src/bridge.rs:571` with "misaligned pointer dereference: address must be a multiple of 0x8 but is 0x442c75c" — the SAME UB that manifested as an opaque `unreachable` in the wasm-opt'd release runtime (wasm hardware tolerates unaligned loads, so it corrupted/trapped downstream instead of faulting at the read). ROOT CAUSE (a CLASS, M05): an aligned 8-byte scalar read (u64/f64/i64) or an 8-aligned struct projection through a raw pointer that ORIGINATES FROM C. On wasm32 a C `PyObject` has struct alignment 4 (its widest member, `Py_ssize_t`==isize==4B / `ob_type`==4B), so a statically-declared C object (module / type / exception singleton) lands on a 4-byte-aligned address; `read_bridge_header_bits` computes the trailer at `base + size_of::<PyObject>()`(==8) and the aligned `*trailer` u64 read is UB. DECISION (the deep question in the task): C mints these pointers and molt does NOT control their alignment, so `core::ptr::read_unaligned` is correct — and because wasm loads carry no alignment penalty, it is also free (no aligned-alloc win available since molt doesn't own the allocation). Molt-minted `BridgeHeader`s DO stay 8-aligned (`Box<BridgeHeader>` + trailing `u64` field forces `align_of==8`); a new `debug_assert_aligned!` helper (lib.rs) at the mint site (`allocate_pyobj_entry`) documents+enforces that so iteration mode flags any drift where molt's own allocation loses alignment. CLASS SWEEP (bridge.rs + api/*.rs + shims, complete): 5 sites of the raw 8-byte-through-C pattern — (1) bridge.rs:571 u64 trailer [FULLY MINE]; (2-4) typeobj.rs PyMember_GetOne T_DOUBLE(f64)/T_LONGLONG(c_longlong)/T_ULONGLONG(c_ulonglong) member reads where `field = addr + C-defined offset` is only object-aligned; (5) numbers.rs PyComplex_AsCComplex `cval` projection off a genuine (non-bridge-minted) C complex object — now a raw field ref (no deref) + read_unaligned. The ≤4-byte reads (i32/u32/c_long/isize/f32) are provably ≥4-aligned (base≥4-aligned + 4-mult offset) and left unchanged; MoltBufferView (only other 8-aligned repr(C) struct) is molt-owned via Rust-local pointers, not a hazard. | MIRI-STRICT-PROVENANCE | Claude/claude-miri-strict-provenance-20260710 | 2026-07-10T21:17:18Z | COMPLETE | Ran the cpython-abi pure-Rust surface under Miri (nightly 1.96.0, miri 0.1.0) as a real UB audit. STRICT-provenance (`-Zmiri-strict-provenance`) is INCOMPATIBLE with the bridge's design: it forbids `with_exposed_provenance` entirely, but the bridge stores genuine C `PyObject*` as integers (raw_py/foreign maps, NaN-box fast path) and reconstructs them — the textbook *exposed* provenance case, inexpressible under strict. So the audit runs Miri's DEFAULT (permissive/exposed) provenance + Tree Borrows + symbolic-alignment, all of which detect real aliasing/OOB/UAF/uninit UB. FINDINGS (all fixed or precisely dispositioned — zero suppression): (A) REAL UB in bridge.rs, FIXED at root — (1) `handle_to_pyobj`/`handle_to_borrowed_pyobj` wrote `ob_refcnt` through a `*mut` derived from a SHARED `&` (`&entry.header.py_obj as *const as *mut`), which carries only read permission (`noalias readonly`) — UB, flagged bridge.rs:297; (2) the bridge hands out MULTIPLE aliasing `*mut PyObject` to the same header and mutates refcounts through them (this IS CPython refcounting), but re-deriving a fresh `&`/`&mut` each call popped previously-handed-out pointers off the borrow stack → later access via an earlier pointer is UB, flagged test_bridge.rs:136. Root-cause fix: header field is now `py_obj: UnsafeCell<PyObject>` (interior mutability — the correct model for memory mutated through aliasing raw pointers); all derivations go through `UnsafeCell::get()` from a shared borrow. `UnsafeCell` is `#[repr(transparent)]` so the `#[repr(C)]` ABI layout + trailing `molt_bits` read are byte-identical; machine-code-equivalent (zero-cost). Green under Stacked Borrows + Tree Borrows + symbolic-alignment. Also made ALL handle-encoding pointer<->integer puns provenance-correct: keys use `.addr()` (strict, no expose); reconstructed addresses use `.expose_provenance()` + `with_exposed_provenance_mut()` (raw_py/foreign values, foreign-custody c_ptr params, pyobject_to_bits/bits_to_pyobject). (B) REAL UAF in tests, FIXED — PyList/PyTuple_SetItem STEAL the item ref on every error path (Py_XDECREF before -1, matching CPython); 4 tests double-freed the item afterward (latent under the no-op mock runtime; Miri caught it at refcount.rs:44). Removed the erroneous post-SetItem decrefs (aligns tests with the steal contract; assert behaviour unchanged — not a weakening). (C) buffer `format` read after PyMemoryView_FromBuffer/PyBuffer_Release trips a Stacked-Borrows tag error (test_object_protocol.rs:148, test_modules.rs:541) but is CLEAN under Tree Borrows (the model Rust is converging on) — a model-dependent finding in buffer.rs/memory.rs (the concurrently-active deadlock-sweep lane's files); documented + handed off, NOT stomped. (D) Miri LIMITATIONS (out of scope, documented): FFI into the cc-compiled C shim (test_pyarg_parse PyArg_ParseTuple, test_exceptions molt_capi_errno); Windows isolation blocking CreateFileW (test_getset reads a header on disk); leak-checker flagging immortal static types + GLOBAL_BRIDGE cache (run with -Zmiri-ignore-leaks); fn-pointer-identity comparisons (test_type_ready_inheritance tp_free==PyObject_Free — Miri synthetic addresses, pass under real cargo test). COVERAGE: 33 test binaries, 27 fully green under Miri (~363 pure-Rust tests, 0 UB) after the fixes; only 2 binaries fully FFI-blocked (Windows Miri reaches the pure-Rust surface broadly, unlike std/OS-heavy crates). Files touched (surgical): runtime/molt-cpython-abi/src/bridge.rs, tests/test_sequences.rs, docs/agent/MIRI_STRICT_PROVENANCE.md. VERIFIED: cargo test -p molt-lang-cpython-abi green (serial + parallel single-binary; a rare cross-binary full-suite flaky SIGSEGV predates + is unaffected by this change — the fix is machine-equivalent); cargo check --target wasm32-wasip1 green; cargo clippy -p molt-lang-cpython-abi -- -D warnings clean (incl. the new significant_drop_in_scrutinee deny); fail_closed_gate.py OK (fail_open_stub=0); check_table_drift.py --check PASS. Buffer analogue of the bridge UnsafeCell fix (A); a 'static format table was rejected — shape/strides are per-buffer and format is copied per-buffer from arbitrary exporters. Model-correct per field = raw for all three. FLAKY SIGSEGV VERDICT: SAME root as C (not a distinct race) — the invalidated descriptor store was a compile-time provenance hazard LLVM could reorder around into_raw's reborrow; the raw-projection fix removes that reborrow. # Solo-Owner Lane Claims

Some lanes must be driven **END-TO-END by ONE agent**. Splitting them across
agents causes exactly the failures this collaboration has hit repeatedly:
colliding edits, trampled landings, and *masked frontiers* (one agent's local
state hiding the real blocker from another). Those lanes are **SOLO lanes**.

Before working a SOLO lane you MUST claim it here. The claim is a git-atomic
lock: because claims are landed by fast-forward (`tools/ff_land.py`), exactly one
agent can win a claim race — the loser's push is refused and it backs off.

This registry is authoritative at `origin/main`. Read it there, not from a stale
checkout (`python tools/tree_drift_check.py --fetch` first).

## SOLO lanes (claim before starting)

- **`E1-WITNESS-TO-GREEN`** — the entire witness closure, owned end-to-end by one
  agent: seal-regen → WASM link → numpy static-lib link → execution →
  `candidate_outputs.npz` → `check_parity.py` PASS. Do NOT split this across
  agents; the seal artifacts, cpython-abi link, and acceptance lane are one
  coherent arc. (See the E1 order-of-operations in the PAUSE HANDOFF /
  `ORCHESTRATION.md`.)

- **`CODEX-B-CACHE-KEY`** — frontend lowering cache-KEY scoping (`cache_fingerprints.py`
  + `frontend_*` + aligned tests) so unrelated CLI/runtime/link edits don't cold-start
  the witness lowering cache. Coherent structural move; one owner. (Orchestrator-held;
  handoff from the prior in-progress agent per the reclaim directive in `ORCHESTRATION.md`.)

- **`REVIEW-5-SCCP-STR-UNICODE`** — adversarial-review finding #5 (P1
  CODEX-CORRECTNESS): SCCP constant-folding of str builtins/methods must use
  CPython code-point semantics, never Rust UTF-8 byte offsets. Single authority:
  `runtime/molt-passes/src/tir/passes/sccp/eval.rs`
  (`eval_concrete_builtin`/`eval_concrete_method`). One owner so the fix + teeth
  land as a coherent cut.

(Add new SOLO lanes here as the orchestrator or a claimant identifies them.)

## Protocol (binding)

**Helper (does steps 1-3 correctly for you):** `python tools/claim_lane.py <lane>
--check` (read-only; exit 0 = claimable, 1 = held → back off) and
`python tools/claim_lane.py <lane> --claim --agent <id> --note "..."` (pre-checks,
appends the row, `ff_land`s, and backs off on a lost race). Log progress/finish
with `--append PROGRESS|COMPLETE|RELEASED --agent <id> --note "..."`. The manual
steps below are the contract it implements.

1. **Drift-sweep first.** `git fetch origin`; read THIS file at current
   `origin/main`. `python tools/tree_drift_check.py --fetch` to confirm your tree
   isn't masking.
2. **Check the claim.** If the SOLO lane you want is `CLAIMED` and not STALE (see
   §6), **BACK OFF** — pick a different unclaimed SOLO lane, or a standing lane
   (Codex B/C/D). Never work a lane another agent holds.
3. **Claim it.** Append ONE row to the log (lane, your agent-id, UTC ISO
   timestamp, `CLAIMED`, first-step note) and land it with
   `python tools/ff_land.py`. Because landing is a fast-forward, exactly ONE
   claim wins a race. **If `ff_land` REFUSES (non-ff), someone moved first** —
   `git fetch`, re-read this file; if your lane is now claimed, discard your claim
   row and BACK OFF.
4. **Own it end-to-end.** While your claim is active, no other agent touches the
   lane's files, artifacts, or tests. Post append-only `PROGRESS` rows (with
   evidence: queue run ids, commits) at least every few hours so the claim is
   visibly alive.
5. **Final completion is NOT a green build.** Mark `COMPLETE` only after ALL of:
   - **(a) FINAL EXIT CRITERIA met, with evidence.** For `E1-WITNESS-TO-GREEN`:
     `candidate_outputs.npz` produced through Molt WASM AND `check_parity.py`
     PASS — cite the `pact-witness-acceptance` queue run id and paste the parity
     verdict. Zero fakes, no host-CPython/Pyodide fallback, POISON-clean.
   - **(b) RECURSIVE ADVERSARIAL REVIEW accomplished.** You (or a reviewer you
     spawn) actively tried to REFUTE the result from independent angles
     (re-run on a clean origin/main worktree; probe determinism/tolerances;
     confirm no masking, no fake symbol, no weakened assert) and it SURVIVED.
     Record what you tried and why it held.
   - **(c) SENIOR-ENGINEER REVIEW green.** Correctness incl. memory safety;
     performance (faster than CPython on the claimed target, honest numbers);
     CPython ≥3.12 compatibility in the verified subset; fidelity; no
     duplicate-authority / no partials. Record the sign-off.
   Only then append a `COMPLETE` row with the evidence links and `ff_land` it.
6. **Stale / release.** A claim with an explicit `RELEASED` row may be reclaimed.
   Otherwise a claim is STALE **only if BOTH** (a) no `PROGRESS` row for >4h AND
   (b) an OBJECTIVE-LIVENESS check shows no real activity. A missing PROGRESS row
   is NOT sufficient — an active agent often works silently in its worktree/queue
   without touching this log (this cost a wrong reclaim 2026-07-08). Before landing
   a `RECLAIM` row you MUST verify NO objective activity in the last ~2h:
   - the claimant's worktree commit log: `git -C <worktree> log --oneline --since="3 hours ago"`;
   - recent proof-queue rows for the lane: `tools/proof_queue.py status` (+ any
     worktree-local `logs/proof_queue`);
   - recent `origin/main` commits by that agent.
   If ANY show recent activity, the claim is ALIVE — do NOT reclaim (post a
   PROGRESS observation instead and ping the claimant to log its own progress).
   Cite the objective-liveness evidence (or its absence) in the `RECLAIM` row.
   Never silently take over a live claim; escalate to the orchestrator if contested.
   **Claimants:** post a `PROGRESS` row (or `python tools/claim_lane.py <lane>
   --append PROGRESS ...`) at least every few hours — silence forces others to
   guess your liveness from commits/queue, which is fragile.
7. **Self-retirement (terminal statuses).** A claim carries the seed of its OWN
   retirement (APPARATUS A11 / M11): besides the positive `COMPLETE` / `RELEASED`,
   a claimant may append an evidence-bearing **terminal** status that FREES the
   lane so stale custody cannot silently block a claimant. Recognized terminals:
   - `FALSIFIED` — the lane's PREMISE was disproven (cite what disproved it).
   - `MEASURED_IMPLEMENTATION_RETIRED` — built AND measured, then retired
     (superseded by a better result; cite the measurement).
   - `STALE_ASSUMED_DEAD` — no objective liveness after the CLAIMS.md §6 checks;
     retired with the liveness evidence (or its absence) cited.
   - `SUPERSEDED` — a different lane subsumed this one (cite the lane/commit).

   Append one via `python tools/claim_lane.py <lane> --append <STATUS> --agent
   <id> --note "<evidence>"`. `python tools/claims_status.py` reports the whole
   ledger's live-vs-retired counts and flags any stale `CLAIMED` lane (advisory,
   never an auto-reclaim); `python tools/check_sister_landed.py --files <targets>`
   is the premise-verification preflight a spawned agent runs BEFORE its first
   Write (exit 8 = STAND_DOWN_DUPLICATE, 9 = WAIT_AND_REASSESS) to avoid
   duplicating a landed lane or colliding with a mid-flight one (M22/M24).

| E1-WITNESS-TO-GREEN | claude/opus-e1-misaligned-ptr-20260710 | 2026-07-10T16:50:00Z | PROGRESS | MISALIGNED-POINTER CLASS FIX LANDED (a98ef2978e, ff on origin/main from fefa53c468; drift+fail_closed+table_drift gates PASS). This is the PRECISE root of row 194's "RuntimeError: unreachable" raw-wasm-trap frontier: iteration mode RUN 20260710T155049 (debug assertions ON) aborted at `runtime/molt-cpython-abi/src/bridge.rs:571` with "misaligned pointer dereference: address must be a multiple of 0x8 but is 0x442c75c" — the SAME UB that manifested as an opaque `unreachable` in the wasm-opt'd release runtime (wasm hardware tolerates unaligned loads, so it corrupted/trapped downstream instead of faulting at the read). ROOT CAUSE (a CLASS, M05): an aligned 8-byte scalar read (u64/f64/i64) or an 8-aligned struct projection through a raw pointer that ORIGINATES FROM C. On wasm32 a C `PyObject` has struct alignment 4 (its widest member, `Py_ssize_t`==isize==4B / `ob_type`==4B), so a statically-declared C object (module / type / exception singleton) lands on a 4-byte-aligned address; `read_bridge_header_bits` computes the trailer at `base + size_of::<PyObject>()`(==8) and the aligned `*trailer` u64 read is UB. DECISION (the deep question in the task): C mints these pointers and molt does NOT control their alignment, so `core::ptr::read_unaligned` is correct — and because wasm loads carry no alignment penalty, it is also free (no aligned-alloc win available since molt doesn't own the allocation). Molt-minted `BridgeHeader`s DO stay 8-aligned (`Box<BridgeHeader>` + trailing `u64` field forces `align_of==8`); a new `debug_assert_aligned!` helper (lib.rs) at the mint site (`allocate_pyobj_entry`) documents+enforces that so iteration mode flags any drift where molt's own allocation loses alignment. CLASS SWEEP (bridge.rs + api/*.rs + shims, complete): 5 sites of the raw 8-byte-through-C pattern — (1) bridge.rs:571 u64 trailer [FULLY MINE]; (2-4) typeobj.rs PyMember_GetOne T_DOUBLE(f64)/T_LONGLONG(c_longlong)/T_ULONGLONG(c_ulonglong) member reads where `field = addr + C-defined offset` is only object-aligned; (5) numbers.rs PyComplex_AsCComplex `cval` projection off a genuine (non-bridge-minted) C complex object — now a raw field ref (no deref) + read_unaligned. The ≤4-byte reads (i32/u32/c_long/isize/f32) are provably ≥4-aligned (base≥4-aligned + 4-mult offset) and left unchanged; MoltBufferView (only other 8-aligned repr(C) struct) is molt-owned via Rust-local pointers, not a hazard. MASK-PROOF (bridge.rs `read_bridge_header_bits_tolerates_4byte_aligned_pointer`): builds a deliberately 4-byte-aligned-not-8 buffer and exercises the trailer path; VERIFIED it aborts pre-fix under debug assertions with the exact frontier signature ("misaligned pointer dereference: address must be a multiple of 0x8", non-unwinding abort at the reverted `*trailer`), passes post-fix. `cargo test -p molt-lang-cpython-abi` green (36 lib + ALL integration binaries incl. test_numbers 46, test_type_operations 17); `cargo check --target wasm32-wasip1` green; clippy 0 warnings on the 4 touched files (the crate's 28 pre-existing warnings are all in untouched code — the set_fn transmute macro at typeobj.rs:894 etc.). | MIRI-STRICT-PROVENANCE | Claude/claude-miri-strict-provenance-20260710 | 2026-07-10T21:17:18Z | COMPLETE | Ran the cpython-abi pure-Rust surface under Miri (nightly 1.96.0, miri 0.1.0) as a real UB audit. STRICT-provenance (`-Zmiri-strict-provenance`) is INCOMPATIBLE with the bridge's design: it forbids `with_exposed_provenance` entirely, but the bridge stores genuine C `PyObject*` as integers (raw_py/foreign maps, NaN-box fast path) and reconstructs them — the textbook *exposed* provenance case, inexpressible under strict. So the audit runs Miri's DEFAULT (permissive/exposed) provenance + Tree Borrows + symbolic-alignment, all of which detect real aliasing/OOB/UAF/uninit UB. FINDINGS (all fixed or precisely dispositioned — zero suppression): (A) REAL UB in bridge.rs, FIXED at root — (1) `handle_to_pyobj`/`handle_to_borrowed_pyobj` wrote `ob_refcnt` through a `*mut` derived from a SHARED `&` (`&entry.header.py_obj as *const as *mut`), which carries only read permission (`noalias readonly`) — UB, flagged bridge.rs:297; (2) the bridge hands out MULTIPLE aliasing `*mut PyObject` to the same header and mutates refcounts through them (this IS CPython refcounting), but re-deriving a fresh `&`/`&mut` each call popped previously-handed-out pointers off the borrow stack → later access via an earlier pointer is UB, flagged test_bridge.rs:136. Root-cause fix: header field is now `py_obj: UnsafeCell<PyObject>` (interior mutability — the correct model for memory mutated through aliasing raw pointers); all derivations go through `UnsafeCell::get()` from a shared borrow. `UnsafeCell` is `#[repr(transparent)]` so the `#[repr(C)]` ABI layout + trailing `molt_bits` read are byte-identical; machine-code-equivalent (zero-cost). Green under Stacked Borrows + Tree Borrows + symbolic-alignment. Also made ALL handle-encoding pointer<->integer puns provenance-correct: keys use `.addr()` (strict, no expose); reconstructed addresses use `.expose_provenance()` + `with_exposed_provenance_mut()` (raw_py/foreign values, foreign-custody c_ptr params, pyobject_to_bits/bits_to_pyobject). (B) REAL UAF in tests, FIXED — PyList/PyTuple_SetItem STEAL the item ref on every error path (Py_XDECREF before -1, matching CPython); 4 tests double-freed the item afterward (latent under the no-op mock runtime; Miri caught it at refcount.rs:44). Removed the erroneous post-SetItem decrefs (aligns tests with the steal contract; assert behaviour unchanged — not a weakening). (C) buffer `format` read after PyMemoryView_FromBuffer/PyBuffer_Release trips a Stacked-Borrows tag error (test_object_protocol.rs:148, test_modules.rs:541) but is CLEAN under Tree Borrows (the model Rust is converging on) — a model-dependent finding in buffer.rs/memory.rs (the concurrently-active deadlock-sweep lane's files); documented + handed off, NOT stomped. (D) Miri LIMITATIONS (out of scope, documented): FFI into the cc-compiled C shim (test_pyarg_parse PyArg_ParseTuple, test_exceptions molt_capi_errno); Windows isolation blocking CreateFileW (test_getset reads a header on disk); leak-checker flagging immortal static types + GLOBAL_BRIDGE cache (run with -Zmiri-ignore-leaks); fn-pointer-identity comparisons (test_type_ready_inheritance tp_free==PyObject_Free — Miri synthetic addresses, pass under real cargo test). COVERAGE: 33 test binaries, 27 fully green under Miri (~363 pure-Rust tests, 0 UB) after the fixes; only 2 binaries fully FFI-blocked (Windows Miri reaches the pure-Rust surface broadly, unlike std/OS-heavy crates). Fix = whole-archive printscan ONLY in wasm_link.py's split_native_app_cmd (NOT the combined cmd; the split app has no reloc runtime so no dup). (2) deploy `molt_runtime.wasm` is a non-reloc cdylib (needs fixed addresses; can't be the reloc runtime) whose cpython-abi shim pulls libc.a's stub; rustc link-order blocks cleaning it (whole-archive dups vfprintf, `-lc-printscan-long-double` lazy is placed after `-lc` so too late). Needs a post-cargo relink of the cdylib OR the shim not referencing the %L path. Handing (1)+(2) to the E1-witness owner; my durability + build-unblock fixes stand. |

ALL are pure test-isolation on process-global state, NOT product bugs: serial `--test-threads=1` was already 0-flake; the ABI's refcount/bridge model assumes the GIL-serialized single-threaded C-extension execution that production always has and cargo's parallel test THREADS (one shared GLOBAL_BRIDGE per binary) violate. ROOTS: (1) small-int proxy eviction -- GLOBAL_BRIDGE dedupes small-int proxies by value with non-atomic ob_refcnt mutated OUTSIDE the bridge lock (Py_INCREF/DECREF); siblings creating the same value (e.g. 2) race the shared refcount to zero mid-test, evicting a live handle so slice_index/RichCompare return -1/NotImplemented (test_slice, test_object_protocol richcompare); (2) shared NEW_CALLS/INIT_CALLS delta-asserted counters + shared PyType_Type (test_type_ready_inheritance metatypes); (3) a process-global #[global_allocator] AtomicUsize counter polluted by other libtest threads breaking the +/-0.01 allocs/export budget (buffer_export_bench). FIX (isolation only -- ZERO product assertions changed; diff is lock/init/counter-infra/comment): each affected binary's init() now takes a file-wide poison-tolerant TEST_LOCK and returns the guard every #[test] binds for its body (the TEST_LOCK convention already used across this crate), restoring the single-threaded invariant; buffer_export_bench's counter moved to a const-init thread_local Cell the allocator bumps (allocation-free, try_with never panics) so the budget measures ONLY its own thread. PROOF: full `cargo test -p molt-lang-cpython-abi --no-fail-fast` 90/90 green (70 pre-rebase + 20 on this base); targeted binary stress test_slice 0/800, test_modules 0/1700, test_type_ready_inheritance 0/400, test_object_protocol 0/300, lib buffer_export_bench 0/150 (all previously flaked, e.g. slice 5/200 at -1 vs 0). clippy --all-targets -D warnings clean; fail_closed_gate OK; check_table_drift PASS. |
